#2127. ‘More misery ahead’ : West Ham vs Stoke City predictions

West Ham had hoped things would get better under Scott Parker, but the new boss is yet to oversee his first win since replacing Nuno Espirito Santo. Things got worse for Parker and WHUFC on Tuesday night as they lost 1-0 to Portsmouth, making it six Championship matches without a win to leave them down in 13th.

The Hammers are the league’s top scorers, and whilst Jarrod Bowen is leading the way with seven goals and seven assists, one surprise goal source has been Konstantinos Mavropanos on six. Strikers are struggling to step up, and Parker was a new lead talisman in January.

On the flip side, the story is different for Mark Robins‘ Stoke City. After a tough start, the Potters look like one of the most complete teams in the Championship. After winning with 10 men against Birmingham City last week, City backed it up with a 2-1 win over Millwall, thanks to a George Hirst brace.

There’s a lot to like about Stoke City, but what really catches the eye is just how well they hold on to a lead. In their last five games, they have taken the lead on four occasions and not dropped a single point.

“West Ham look a side made up of individuals rather than a team. That’s the problem that Scott Parker has to solve if he is to mould the Hammers into a side like his other previous promotion-winning outfits. Doing that against high-flying Stoke City does not look like the place to start.

“Mark Robins has his Potters team flying and looking good value for their 2nd-place position. Stoke City are so hard to score against, and if they take the lead, it’s almost game over. This team look the real deal and are only going in one direction.

“WHUFC have the quality to win this game, but not the togetherness. It will all come down to who scores first, and Stoke usually do.”

“Most people would have thought West Ham would be the side sat in 2nd, while Stoke City languish in 13th. However, with 17 games of the season down, WHUFC are struggling for form, while the Potters just can’t stop winning.

“Given the talent on offer, it does feel like a matter of when the Hammers get going, rather than if. With his pedigree in the Championship and stars like Jarrod Bowen on the books, Scott Parker will find a winning formula. That said, four defeats in a six-game winless run is understandably worrying supporters.

“The current situation at Stoke could not be much more different. They are absolutely up on cloud nine at the moment, with five Championship wins on the trot lifting them into 2nd. The numbers might suggest a dip in form is coming, but when you’re tight at the back and have goals coming from multiple sources, you’re going to win games.

“The London Stadium is not a happy place at the moment, and with SCFC coming into the game in such confidence, you have to think the visitors win again with more misery ahead for Parker and co.”

#2126. Longhi sulla Lazio: «Isaksen mi ricorda Robben», poi elogia tutta la squadra

Il commentatore apprezza anche Taylor e Nuno Tavares, prima di confrontare Roma e Inter nella corsa in campionato.

Bruno Longhi elogia la Lazio per il suo gioco e per la disposizione dei calciatori in campo. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, Intervenuto a Maracanà sui temi della Serie A, ha detto: «Attenzione però che io sono innamorato della Lazio, la vedo giocare ed è uno spettacolo. Ha i giocatori giusti al posto giusto. Non la metterei in disparte.»

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Longhi ha poi indicato alcuni dei giocatori che apprezza nella squadra biancoceleste: «La Lazio ha tutti giocatori che mi piacciono. Isaksen mi ricorda Robben, poi Taylor, Nuno Tavares, una squadra dove tutti giocano bene ed è un piacere vederla». Il paragone con Robben riguarda dunque Isaksen, mentre il giudizio positivo si estende al gioco dell’intera squadra.

Parlando della Roma, Longhi ha definito inatteso il segnale arrivato dalla squadra. Ha richiamato City-Napoli, partita in cui a suo giudizio aveva prevalso il tatticismo, e l’ha contrapposta alla gara giocata a viso aperto sul campo del PSG, che nelle sue parole domina da due anni in Champions. La Roma, ha osservato, ha affrontato l’impegno come se avesse davanti un’altra squadra di Serie A.

Longhi ha descritto la formazione giallorossa attraverso forza, temperamento, pressing e tecnica. Di Gasperini ha detto che ha giocato sul campo del PSG scegliendo i calciatori da impiegare senza preoccupazioni: per lui è il segnale di una rosa adatta a qualsiasi tipo di impegno.

Sull’Inter il giudizio è diverso: «Ha disputato 17 partite e non si è mai vista l'Inter come lo scorso anno o di due anni fa, per vari motivi. L'Inter rimane forte, però rimane indecifrabile.» Per questo, nel confronto di campionato, Longhi indica più la Roma dei nerazzurri.

#2122. ‘Been written off’ : Blackburn Rovers vs Charlton Athletic predictions

Despite Tony Mowbray stepping back from his role as head coach of Blackburn Rovers, the side has maintained form with four points in their last two games, including a 1-0 win over Watford on Tuesday night. The points have the East Lancashire side looking up rather than down, sitting in 12th and only two points off the Championship play-offs.

Club captain Todd Cantwell has stepped up impressively for Rovers, netting in each of the last two games to lead by example in a challenging time at Ewood Park. It’s not been easy for the 28-year-old over the last few years, but he is really showing his worth in his third season at the club.

Nathan Jones‘ Charlton Athletic returned to winning ways as they dismantled Lincoln City 4-0, a result that saw both Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw lose their jobs. There’s no hiding that the Addicks will be in and around the Championship relegation fight this season, so beating those sides that will also be down there is of the utmost importance.

The victory displayed just what the Addicks can do, and they will be eager to produce a similar performance this weekend. For Jones’, it is all about pace from the wings, with Tyreece Campbell leading most attacks and delivering six goals from only 2.24 xG in an impressive 2026/27 campaign.

“Blackburn Rovers have consistently been written off this season and have consistently hit back. After losing Tony Mowbray, many thought they would fall off, and instead they have continued on the same course. Rovers don’t have all the quality in the world, but they have composure and control, and they showed real maturity in seeing out their win over Watford.

“Charlton Athletic are an odd entity – completely hot and cold, yet picking up enough points to stay above water. It’s hard to know what they’ll do week on week but heading to Ewood Park is a challenge for any team, regardless of where they sit in the league.

“Even though the Addicks are unpredictable, this looks like a game the hosts can, and should, win.”

“Blackburn Rovers got back to winning ways on Wednesday night, defeating Watford after drawing with rivals Burnley. That will feel like an important win, as it was the first without Tony Mowbray in the dugout after he stepped back from his head coach role for health reasons.

“Rovers have got a pretty good home record this season too, winning five of their eight Ewood Park games. That combined with the form of star attackers Andri Gudjohnsen and Todd Cantwell, who has scored in back-to-back games, should mean they’re confident of beating Charlton Athletic.

“The Addicks will be in a confident mood themselves, though. They put Lincoln City to the sword with a welcome display of ruthless attacking and have now won two of their last three, easing some of the pressure on Nathan Jones and putting some distance between them and the bottom three.

“The issue for the visitors is that since beating West Ham in their first away league game of the season, they have taken just two points on the road from seven games. For that reason, I’m leaning towards another Rovers victory here.”

#2121. AC Milan 5-1 Frosinone: Counter-attacking clinic sees Rossoneri run riot

AC Milan put on a show for their fans at San Siro on Sunday evening as they beat Frosinone 5-1 to move back into the top four.

It was a game that promised entertainment from the early stages, and Milan took command thanks to an own-goal from Gabriele Calvani and a much-needed goal from Goncalo Ramos, before Patrizio Masini scored to halve the deficit.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Rossoneri ran away with things in the second half, with Ardon Jashari and Alphadjo Cisse netting off the bench before Christian Pulisic got a goal of his own to add some gloss to the scoreline. There were chances to make it an even bigger margin, but five would have to do.

Given that Lazio, Roma, Como and Juventus had all won prior to kick-off, it was important that Milan matched their results to keep up the pressure at the top end of the table, and they did so in emphatic fashion.

Ruben Amorim made just one change to the starting line-up from the side that won against Genoa before the break, with Alphadjo Cisse dropping out and Davide Bartesaghi coming in. It was uncertain from the line-up named what the system would be, as has often been the case with the Portuguese coach so far.

Frosinone started the game better and called Mike Maignan into action inside the first minute with a close-range header that was saved, though the flag was up. Then, a deflected strike from Masini on the edge of the box ended up scraping the upright and going behind for a corner.

However, it was Milan who struck first at San Siro. Luka Modric deserves the plaudits for an outstanding through ball to find Adrien Rabiot’s run in behind, and the Frenchman’s square pass was heading towards Christian Pulisic’s run but was turned into his own net by Calvani, leaving Palmisani rooted.

The frantic pace of the early stages continued, as a lovely exchange between Diego Moreira and Pulisic saw a threatening cross turned behind. A few minutes later, Yunus Musah found Moreira who had a shot deflected for a corner, which Goncalo Ramos rose and met well but headed over the bar.

Frosinone kept providing reminders that they too pose a threat going forward, such as on the quarter-hour mark when Kvernadze surged down the left and got off a low shot from a narrow angle that Maignan had to parry behind. The corner wasn’t properly dealt with, and Cittadini flashed one across the six-yard box that Rabiot had to put into the stands.

Just like with the first goal, Milan punished Frosinone for not making the most of their spell of pressure. Goncalo Ramos was the man who started and finished a flowing counter-attack, robbing Calvani of possession before running towards goal. Musah’s through ball was run onto by the electric Moreira, who found Rabiot, and the Frenchman’s cut-back was tucked away by the Portuguese.

The two-goal lead was very quickly halved though, as Frosinone got the goal their pressure had threatened. Kvernadze’s run down the left and cut-back from the byline was scuffed onwards by Ghedjemis into the path of Masini, who toe-poked it in at the far post.

In the 34th minute, there was a moment that enraged the Milan players, staff and fans. The referee blew his whistle for a shirt pull on Rabiot just as Milan went through on goal with two men in behind and just the goalkeeper to beat. The official apologised, but it felt like a certain goal had been taken away.

It was a half played at a hundred miles an hour, and it ended that way too. Pulisic flashed an effort over the bar from a good position, while Davide Bartesaghi’s low cross towards Ramos was cut out in the nick of time as Milan continued to get joy down the flanks.

Amorim opted to make an intriguing change at half-time, bringing off Bartesaghi and replacing him with Ardon Jashari. This change in turn saw Musah shift to left-back, a role that he played against Genoa in the last match, and from minute one.

The change proved to be an absolute masterstroke, as Jashari scored inside three minutes. A ball was sent long to Ramos who knocked it down to Rabiot, whose first touch was a lovely through ball to the Swiss midfield. It looked like he had overrun it with the square option not used, but he lashed a shot in at the near post.

Not long after the goal, Ramos gestured towards the bench that he felt some discomfort. He wanted to play through it but Amorim took no risks, opting to bring Alphadjo Cisse on to lead the line.

There was a rather rare occurrence in the 54th minute, as Milan were awarded an indirect free-kick after Palmisani picked up a back pass from Calvani. Modric laid it off to Rabiot, but the goalkeeper made up for his error with a good save.

Continuing the trend of impact substitutions, Cisse got his ninth goal of the season right on the hour mark. A Frosinone corner was cleared and Pulisic found Moreira who had the Italian for company, and he finished the through ball by the Belgian past Palmisani with his trademark composure. The offside flag went up, but after a long check the goal was given.

Milan continued to look like scoring every time they got out of their own half, and Pulisic joined the fun in the 68th minute with a goal of his own. Frosinone again left one man back for a corner that was cleared, allowing the American to easily outpace Terzic with a run from inside his own half, dinking over Palmisani to make it 5-1.

With 20 minutes left on the clock, Amorim brought Strahinja Pavlovic and Pervis Estupinan on for Diawara and Musah. The final change saw Omari Hutchinson replace Pulisic.

Pavlovic looked far from 100% when he came on, appearing dizzy at certain moments. The head coach seemed to suggest coming off so as not to risk things, but he played on.

Milan had chances to make the scoreline even more impressive, with Moreira and Hutchinson squandering a couple of chances on the break. However, at full-time the team were rightly applauded by the San Siro crowd.

Just learn some football don’t follow statistics. That kind of open football without proper midfield and scoring options are destined to be exposed especiallyin Serie A. Frosinone is a solid team and rather defensive too. So this was a really impressive performance by the lads. Everyone got in the scoring action and it could have been by a even wider score.

We had one or two issues defensively, but this is a team that is performing and responding. The fact that many of these goals were on counters shows how potent we can be when focused on winning back the ball.
